# DF — review round 6: content addresses and numeric/type admission

Branch: `plans/ea-w2-filesafe-20261104`. Reviewed baseline: `233112bf6aa6f47bb1c58bf36bb7e6731625c07a`. Assessed repair and proof: `8a2cbfbb23710541235f49ad53139ae80d63720f`. The subsequent report/self-grade commit changes no assessed source.

All nine families pass as prepared without admission. The seven previously passing families retain their contracts and original controls. C1 stays ABSENT, seven C7 cells stay PARTIAL, and native execution stays NOT_RUN. B1 and S1 are closed; the seven narrowed retention questions remain pending for Jared.

## Review round 6: findings and responses

- **B1 — fixed (`8a2cbfbb23710541235f49ad53139ae80d63720f`). Where:** `scripts/pm_filesafe_event_authority.py`, `application_original_failures` and `ProtocolOracle.read_body`; CV-557/CV-558 and ATS-279. **Cause:** declared BLAKE3 addresses selected bytes without recomputing their address. **Fix:** recompute every resolved file blob address and compare both inventory and manifest IDs, preserving SHA-256, lengths, inventory coverage and payload-store selection. Coherent forged-address, swapped-blob and truncated-blob negatives repair every dependent manifest/source/receipt/issued copy. The existing SHA-256 negative now preserves a genuine BLAKE3 address/bytes so the new guard cannot mask it. Verify the two actual reviewer counterexamples, own fixtures and literal removal rows.

- **S1 / Astra item 10 — fixed (`8a2cbfbb23710541235f49ad53139ae80d63720f`). Where:** current materialization, retained issued numeric admission, SP-337 descriptor resolution and all nine own CV/source-schema contracts. **Cause:** mathematical integer admission and Python equality allowed different typed hash inputs. **Fix:** current source/event/custody admission refuses all floating numbers before hashes/joins, independently selected writer epochs require exact ints, and parsed SP-337 descriptor numbers are checked too. `1.0`, `1e0`, `-0.0`, floating live epochs and Project/application floating reference sizes have coherent negatives; `1`, `0` and parsed `-0` have controls, and malformed `01` refuses in JSON parsing. Existing source commitment recipes are unchanged: historical sources verify the typed recipe they were written with, without current-writer admission or restamping.

- **Item 11 sweep — fixed (`8a2cbfbb23710541235f49ad53139ae80d63720f`). Where:** source-graph stage ordinals, source/reader generation predicates and retained source/issued typed ingress. **Cause:** Python bool/float equality and dereferences before exact typed ingress. **Fix:** require exact integer graph ordinals and independently selected generations, validate issued event/custody roots before reading them, and validate retained sources before commitment comparisons. String booleans/numbers, null, arrays, nested objects and boolean epochs have own typed controls. Legacy mathematically integral source epochs retain their original historical recipe.

- **N1 — integration note, unchanged.** Index validation exits 1/top-level fail with 22 failures: 21 byte-identical legacy decision-ledger inputs plus later-main DL-302. No stale generated index failures. DF deletes no Decision Log text; integration must preserve newer mainline canon and regenerate merged derivatives. No unrelated ledger is edited and no claim of green repository-wide index validation is made.

## Guard proof and boundaries

`python3 scripts/pm_filesafe_guard_isolation.py --jobs 1 --progress --output <evidence-path>` executes **846 guards × 3466 negatives = 2,932,236 literal mutant evaluations**, one guard or named duplicate set at a time. There is no trace-proof/own-only bypass. All 3119 prior control IDs remain. Zero uncovered guards, unexpected flips, exceptions or unchanged-trace shortcuts. Minimum available memory before a guard was 6 GiB; the driver stops below 6 GiB. Its OS process was also suspended/resumed near the threshold to preserve every completed row; memory-monitor.log retains those transitions.

The table has 839 single-removal rows and 7 named duplicate-set rows. The new duplicate set is the original byte-SHA historical-body check plus complete original decoding: both reject the same changed application body. Each set names its members and proves joint removal. Every own target flips; all remaining negatives reject except explicitly listed related upper-layer flips dependent on the same removed predicate. Schema ingress, primitive type/codec predicates, complete materialization, issued helpers and complete protocol/body reads are labelled separately; an exception is never acceptance.

Event-only history/retry/rebuild reads issued EventRecords and receipts, which contain no snapshot blob address to resolve. They remain readable after original controls are disposed. Every path that resolves snapshot bytes uses the verified original composition; body-dependent historical reads reuse the same BLAKE3 verifier without a live-writer epoch check. Restore execution, actual physical packs, permissions/holds, native durability and CAS remain authored NOT_RUN.

## Eleven standing checks

| Check | Round-6 evidence / result |
|---|---|
| 1 — replay-only current writes | Original own writer/retry negatives retained; compatibility/event-only history remains readable. |
| 2 — application keys | CV-366 encoded application keys and family identity vectors retained; coherent foreign keys refuse. |
| 3 — Project partition | All positives retain encoded `project~` partitions; raw-ID negatives remain. |
| 4 — 16 KiB / SP-337 | Exact ASCII/UTF-8 limits, full-body measurement and durable BLAKE3 references retained; parsed reference sizes now require integers. |
| 5 — LF identifiers | Full-length/full-match schema controls and independent LF mutations retained and removed in the literal matrix. |
| 6 — retry semantics | Storage-assigned observation/sequence metadata stays outside caller-semantic comparison; exact original issued result/first receipt remains returned. |
| 7 — checkpoint identity | Each own consumer adopts SP-278 with family_checkpoint=none_required; foreign Storage identity and generation controls refuse. Native CAS NOT_RUN. |
| 8 — failure exit | Positive-as-negative and removed-guard full-entry controls require failure status/nonzero exit. |
| 9 — all refs | Complete offline resource closure plus all own unused/nested dangling-reference negatives retained. |
| 10 — retained history | Original inline/reference, epoch rollover, disposal, withdrawal and forgery traces retained; old typed source-epoch recipe controls added. |
| 11 — isolation | Entire literal guard table rerun, with named duplicates and no masked own negative, exception or uncovered guard. |

## Eleven recurring Astra classes

| Class | How ruled out |
|---|---|
| 1 — forged/contradictory inputs | Own joined identity/source/receipt/manifest/outcome negatives remain. New B1 negatives and the reviewer’s two exact witnesses now refuse recomputed addresses. |
| 2 — Storage metadata | Independent issued custody remains authoritative; metadata-only retries return its original record, while altered receipt/Storage identity and generation types refuse. |
| 3 — historical readers | Event-only reads survive live epoch changes/disposal; body requests still require retained typed source/body custody. Old typed numeric source recipes stay readable, withdrawal/forgery still refuses. |
| 4 — inconsistent reads | Typed ingress precedes joins/dereferences; parsed descriptors, source hashes and independent generations cannot interpret one accepted scalar differently. Existing hash case/space/LF negatives retained. |
| 5 — unresolved refs | Offline eager closure visits unused definitions and nested resources; both entry points retain all dangling-reference controls. |
| 6 — partial size limits | Original complete-body ASCII/UTF-8 boundaries and SP-337 routes retained; no duplicate large stored EventRecord body introduced. Original composition views retain existing physical owners. |
| 7 — path custody | Original selected source, issued/receipt recovery and survivor fences retained. Every actual blob-byte resolution recomputes BLAKE3/SHA-256/length; passive event-row reads do not invent disposed bodies. |
| 8 — masked negatives | All prior IDs retained; repaired SHA-256 negative and new address/numeric cases have no stale dependent commitments. Full literal matrix and named duplicate sets prove stated boundaries. |
| 9 — inflated grades | C12 upgrades only after the complete proof; two C8 upgrades cite repaired own custody text. C1 absent, seven C7 partial, native NOT_RUN. |
| 10 — numeric forms | Current floats refuse before commitments, including numbers decoded from references; graph/counter types are exact. `-0` normalizes once to zero; malformed `01` refuses. Retained source recipe is unchanged. |
| 11 — types | Full typed ingresses reject string booleans/numbers, null, arrays, nested objects and boolean epochs. Issued roots validate before dereference; graph/counter booleans/floats have own removal controls. |

## Checks and scope

Final family-by-family runs: authority {'positive': 158, 'negative': 1891, 'protocol': 1197}; payload {'positive': 94, 'negative': 198, 'identity': 18}. Sweep: 2526 checks, zero failures. Shard generate/check pass (99 sources, 3,092 bodies); index generation passes, validation remains the 22 unrelated N1 failures. `git diff --check` and protected-path/source-preservation checks pass.

DL-045 still covers these nine exact names. Existing F2-213, CV-365/CV-366, SP-286/CV-339 full-value v2 and SP-278/DL-076 bindings remain; their current per-family search evidence is in own SP-550..558. Case L-3 still governs the two resolved exact-kind retention assignments. No premise failed and no new producer, consumer, interface, identity recipe, stored family or lifetime was minted.

IDs reused: **CV-550..558, SP-550..558, ATS-275..279**. No new PlanUnit IDs; CV-559/SP-559 remain unused. No registry/admission/Decision Log, `.gitignore`, governance lock/evidence/auto-decisions, frozen-validator or unrelated test/ledger edit. Only CV-550..558 and ATS-275..279 owner prose changed; regenerated shards belong only to Contracts_V0 and Automated_Testing_System. All original positives/protocols and negative fixture/control IDs remain. No main push, landing lock, mail or subagent.

## Seven retention questions — unchanged

SP-552's authority-indefinite assignment and SP-556's non-authority operational assignment remain justified by current Case L-3 (`Plans/storage-plan.md:17403–17417`) and the exact existing event kinds. The remaining questions, copied from their current own SP paragraphs, remain unanswered:

| Family | Pending question |
|---|---|
| `filesafe.command_denied` | Does the retained command-denial EventRecord constitute audit authority (RP-AUTHORITY-INDEFINITE), or non-authority security diagnostic history (RP-OPERATIONAL-2555D)? |
| `filesafe.destructive_override_denied` | Does the retained destructive-override refusal EventRecord constitute an authority decision receipt (RP-AUTHORITY-INDEFINITE), or non-authority security diagnostic history (RP-OPERATIONAL-2555D)? |
| `filesafe.destructive_override_requested` | Does the retained destructive-override request EventRecord itself constitute approval/audit authority (RP-AUTHORITY-INDEFINITE), or non-authority security diagnostic history (RP-OPERATIONAL-2555D)? |
| `filesafe.guard_init_failed` | Does the retained fail-closed guard-initialization EventRecord constitute audit authority (RP-AUTHORITY-INDEFINITE), or non-authority security diagnostic history (RP-OPERATIONAL-2555D)? |
| `filesafe.path_denied` | Does the retained path-denial EventRecord constitute audit authority (RP-AUTHORITY-INDEFINITE), or non-authority security diagnostic history (RP-OPERATIONAL-2555D)? |
| `safe_point.created` | Is safe_point.created EventRecord history an authority/capture receipt (RP-AUTHORITY-INDEFINITE), non-authority recovery operational history (RP-OPERATIONAL-2555D), or runtime history (RP-RUNTIME-365D), independently of the already-governed released snapshot bodies? |
| `safe_point.restored` | Is safe_point.restored EventRecord history an authority/outcome receipt (RP-AUTHORITY-INDEFINITE), non-authority recovery operational history (RP-OPERATIONAL-2555D), or runtime history (RP-RUNTIME-365D), independently of the already-governed original restore transaction and snapshot bodies? |

These concern the separate retained EventRecords. Existing original input, snapshot, journal, body, generic index and checkpoint owners/lifetimes remain governed by their own contracts.
